{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JK7deQ7fdPovFvE37RNicd","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Healogics, Inc. — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-15\n     4→Developer website: http://www.healogics.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Healogics, Inc. is the nation's largest provider of advanced wound care services, headquartered in Jacksonville, Florida. Founded in 1996, the company operates over 600 hospital-based outpatient Wound Care Centers across the United States — roughly one-third of all hospital outpatient wound care centers in the country. Healogics treats more than 300,000 patients annually and has healed nearly 4 million wounds over its 30-year history. The company employs approximately 3,000 people and works with about 4,000 affiliated physicians plus a Healogics Specialty Physician practice group of nearly 300.\n     9→\n    10→Healogics is not a traditional EHR vendor selling software to external customers. Rather, it is a wound care services company that built its own proprietary EHR (i-heal) for use within its own network of managed wound care centers. The company partners with hospitals to operate wound care centers within their facilities, and also partners with over 300 skilled nursing facilities and provides inpatient consults at more than 80 partner hospitals.\n    11→\n    12→**Ownership history:** Healogics was acquired by Clayton, Dubilier & Rice (CD&R) from Metalmark Capital and Scale Venture Partners in 2014 in a transaction valued at $910 million. At the time, the company had approximately $300 million in revenue and 2,000 employees. In 2015, Healogics acquired Accelecare Wound Centers from Revelstoke Capital Partners, expanding its network. In 2021, the company announced a $240 million equity investment to support future growth. CD&R remains the current owner.\n    13→\n    14→Healogics also maintains one of the world's largest wound-specific longitudinal outcomes databases, containing data on more than five million wounds, which it uses for research, evidence-based care protocols, and quality measurement through its Wound Science Initiative.\n    15→\n    16→## Product: i-heal\n    17→\n    18→CHPL ID: 10140\n    19→\n    20→### What It Is\n    21→\n    22→i-heal 2.0 is Healogics' proprietary, web-based electronic medical records (EMR) and disease management system designed specifically for wound care. It is not sold as a standalone product on the open market — it is used internally across Healogics' network of 600+ Wound Care Centers. The system integrates wound science into clinical workflows to support clinical decision making, simplify documentation, and reinforce the use of evidence-based medicine.\n    23→\n    24→i-heal is one component of a broader software suite called **WoundSuite**, which comprises three applications:\n    25→\n    26→1. **i-heal** — the core documentation management / EMR system, focused on clinical documentation, accurate claims, and maximum reimbursements\n    27→2. **Clinical Optimization** — automates clinical processes aligned with Healogics' Patient Care Process (a six-sigma lean productivity methodology), including strategic scheduling, productivity planning, pre-clinic huddles, intake/discharge assignments, staff communication, physician/case manager collaboration, an Interdisciplinary Care Management Guide (ICMG), and Medical Surveillance Review (MSR) process\n    28→3. **Healogics Photo+** — a mobile application for digital wound measurement, wound photography, and access to evidence-based data. Uses planimetric measurement to capture wound dimensions within seconds, supports pre- and post-debridement documentation, and uploads images to the cloud with HIPAA compliance\n    29→\n    30→The certified module (i-heal 2.0) has a broad set of ONC certifications covering clinical data (CPOE, demographics, problem list, medication list, medication allergy list, clinical decision support, drug interaction checks, implantable device list), transitions of care (ToC send/receive, C-CDA creation/incorporation), patient portal (view/download/transmit), clinical quality measures (CQM reporting), public health reporting (direct project), and FHIR API access.\n    31→\n    32→### Users & Market\n    33→\n    34→i-heal is used by wound care physicians, clinicians, case managers, and front office support staff across the Healogics network. The SED intended user description is: \"Ambulatory, Wound Care Physicians, Clinicians and Front Office Support Staff.\"\n    35→\n    36→The system is deployed across 600+ Wound Care Centers nationwide, with more than 330,000 patients treated annually through the platform. It is used in every patient encounter across the Healogics network. The primary clinical settings are hospital-based outpatient wound care centers, though Healogics also has inpatient programs and partnerships with skilled nursing facilities.\n    37→\n    38→Healogics is not competing in the general EHR market — i-heal is a captive, internal-use system. There are no external customer reviews on platforms like G2 or Capterra for i-heal specifically. The closest competitor product would be Net Health's WoundExpert, which is a commercially available wound care EHR.\n    39→\n    40→Notably, Healogics has partnered with Net Health since 2012 and expanded the relationship in 2023 to integrate Net Health's Tissue Analytics (an AI-powered wound imaging platform) into Healogics' operations. This suggests that while i-heal handles core EMR/documentation, Healogics supplements it with third-party wound imaging and analytics technology.\n    41→\n    42→### Modules & Functionality\n It is used in every patient encounter across the Healogics network. The primary clinical settings are hospital-based outpatient wound care centers, though Healogics also has inpatient programs and partnerships with skilled nursing facilities.\n\nHealogics is not competing in the general EHR market — i-heal is a captive, internal-use system. There are no external customer reviews on platforms like G2 or Capterra for i-heal specifically. The closest competitor product would be Net Health's WoundExpert, which is a commercially available wound care EHR.\n\nNotably, Healogics has partnered with Net Health since 2012 and expanded the relationship in 2023 to integrate Net Health's Tissue Analytics (an AI-powered wound imaging platform) into Healogics' operations. The export archive contains\nclinical documentation entered for each patient into over 70 forms; the archive\ndocuments are delivered in machine readable xml format and combined into a .zip file\nthat can be downloaded from i-heal.\nThis document describes the format of the content along with the definition of common\nelements that can be used to extract and relate data with the patient, visit, associated\ndocuments, signatures, and photos / files. A companion document, EHIExport – Data\nDictionary, provides a data dictionary and identifies the elements within each document.\n\nHIPAA\nAs part of its use as an Electronic Health Record (EHR) management system, ePHI is\ncollected and stored within i-heal 2.0. The Electronic Health Information (EHI) data export\nextracts and exports ePHI; the archive delivered contains proprietary and protected\ninformation as well as reference links containing access tokens that can retrieve protected\ninformation as defined by HIPAA.\nUnder the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act of 1996 (HIPAA), Healogics,\nInc. and its subsidiaries (Healogics, Inc.) are \"Business Associates\" of each Hospital to\nwhich they provide wound care management services and, as such, are statutorily and\ncontractually obligated and liable for maintaining the privacy and security of the\n\"protected health information\" (known as \"PHI\") for all patients treated in the Hospital's\nWound Healing Program (Program). Under the HIPAA \"Privacy Rule\", \"Security Rule\"\n\"HITECH Rule\", and under each Hospital management agreement, Healogics, Inc. is also\nrequired to have comprehensive policies and procedures for the use and disclosure of PHI\nand located on the Healogics WebPortal within the Policy tab at\nhttps://www.dcswebportal.com.\n\n800.411.6281 | nethealth.com\n\n\fI-HEAL 2.0\n\nIn accordance with Healogics policies, by retrieving EHI data exports using the i-heal\n2.0 data export feature, you agree to securely store and protect unauthorized or\nunnecessary access to the export archives and the data access reference links contained\nwithin.\n\nDisclaimer\nWhile Net Health makes every effort to deliver all electronic health records for the\nrequested patient(s), some data may be inaccessible or missing from the delivered archive\ndue to unforeseen or unavoidable reasons. As the recipient of the export EHI records, it is\nyour responsibility to validate the content is complete. Please, notify Healogics, inc. of any\nmissing or inaccurate information; however, neither Net Health nor Healogics can be held\nliable for any missing or inaccurate information.\n\nEHI Data Requests\nElectronic Health Information (EHI) data export requests for a facility are made using a\nfeature in i-heal 2.0 accessible to users having the Facility Administration user role or the\nEmergency Access user role when activated.\nEHI data export may be requested for either a single patient or all patients within the\nfacility. The EHI data export archive may be downloaded from the i-heal 2.0 EHI Data\nExport feature once the request has been processed and is completed, usually within 48\nhours.\n\nExport Archive Format\nElectronic Health Information (EHI) data is exported for each patient grouped into\nnumerous “document” archives for each condition, visit, and EHI data type (i.e. wound,\nvital signs, wound assessment, progress note, etc).\nEach “document” archive is extracted as a separate file in XML format having a filename of\n<documentType>_<documentId>.xml where <documentType> designates the type of\ninformation and <documentId> designates the unique identifier of the data instance.\nAll document archive files for a patient are grouped in a folder having a folder name\n<patientId>, i-heal 2.0’s internal identifier for the patient. A folder is present for each\npatient in the request. The folders and files are packaged, compressed, and delivered in a\nzip formatted file.\n\n800.411.6281 | nethealth.com\n\n\fI-HEAL 2.0\n\nFigure 1 EHI Data Export Zip File Content\n\nExport Data File Format\nElectronic Health Information (EHI) data for each patient is grouped into numerous\n“document” archive files for each condition, visit, and EHI data type (i.e. wound, vital signs,\nwound assessment, progress note, etc) and stored using xml format. 90%;\"><br />\nStandards Version Advancement Process (SVAP) Notices<br />\n<u>Notice of Intent to Implement HL7 FHIR US Core Implementation Guide STU 4.0.0 for 170.315(g)(10)</u><br />\nIssued: January 30, 2023<br />\nPlease be advised that Healogics. Inc. has opted to leverage ONC&#8217;s SVAP process for the certification criteria at 170.315(g)(10) to adopt the HL7 FHIR US Core Implementation Guide STU 4.0.0 in its Patient and Population API Services. The adoption of the updated version will provide better forward compatibility and longevity for the APIs certified to this criterion. Because Healogics, Inc. is adopting this version in its initial certification of to the (g)(10) criterion, it is expected that the adoption of the updated version will have no impact on the interoperability of the affected module.<br />\n</span></p>\n\t\t\t</div>\n\t\t</div>\n\t\t<div class=\"page-header-image\">\n\t\t\t\t<img data-interchange=\"[https://www.healogics.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/research-team-data-overlay-600x374.jpg, small], [https://www.healogics.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/research-team-data-overlay-800x498.jpg, medium], [https://www.healogics.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/research-team-data-overlay-1200x748.jpg, large], [https://www.healogics.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/research-team-data-overlay.jpg, xlarge]\" alt=\"\">\n\t</div>\n</div>\t\t<nav class=\"breadcrumbs-container\">\n\t<div class=\"grid-container-xl\">\n\t\t<div class=\"grid-x\">\n\t\t\t<div class=\"cell\">\n\t\t\t\t<div id=\"breadcrumbs\" class=\"breadcrumbs\"><span><span><a href=\"https://www.healogics.com/\">Home</a></span> » <span class=\"breadcrumb_last\" aria-current=\"page\">Certified EHR Technology</span></span></div>\t\t\t</div>\n\t\t</div>\n\t</div>\n</nav>\t\t\t</main>\n\n\n\t\t\t\t<!-- footer -->\n\t\t\t\t<footer  class=\"footer\" role=\"contentinfo\">\n\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"grid-container-xl\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"grid-x grid-padding-x\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"cell medium-6 footer-cta\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<div class=\"footer-cta-content\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t<h2>Request an Appointment at a Wound Care Center<sup>®</sup> near you.</h2>\n<p>Healogics is the expert in wound healing. The\nframework supports apps for use by clinicians, patients, and others via a Patient Portal or any\nFHIR system where a user can give permissions to launch an App. It provides a reliable, secure\nauthorization protocol for a variety of app architectures, including apps that run on an enduser’s device as well as apps that run on a secure server.\nThis documentation is intended for use by third-party application developers which will\ndescribe registration, syntax, functionality, and errors/exceptions they will see when using the\nFHIR API to integrate with provider(s) using the i-heal 2.0 software.\nRegistration\nDevelopers wishing to integrate with the API must contact the PHI Provider that uses i-heal 2.0\nas their EMR. I-heal 2.0 is an EMR SaaS solution and as such, each wound care center is\nconsidered a separate entity and will have its API URL, registration for which third-party\ndevelopers have access to their data via the FHIR API on their system, and each wound care\ncenter is responsible for maintaining the rights of API accounts. Once a wound care center\ndecides to grant access to you as a third-party developer, they will set up a new developer\naccount with access to the API based on the needs of the developer and agreed-upon\npermissions with the Provider.\nConfigurations\nThird-party developer applications must meet the following configuration settings:\n1. The App MUST read and parse JSON responses.\n2. The App MUST assure that sensitive information (authentication secrets,\nauthorization codes, tokens) are transmitted ONLY to authenticated servers, over TLSsecured channels. §170.315(g)(10) requires secure connection using TLS version 1.2\nor higher.\n3. If the App is a Bulk Export application, the application MAY need to be setup to handle\nlonger lasting connections based on the data that is being exported.\n4. Key findings:\n\n**Export mechanism:** The EHI export is a feature built into i-heal 2.0, accessible to users with Facility Administration or Emergency Access roles. It supports export of a single patient or all patients at a facility. The export is generated asynchronously (\"usually within 48 hours\") and downloaded as a ZIP file from within i-heal.\n\n**Export format:** The export uses a **proprietary XML format** — not FHIR, not C-CDA, not CSV. Each patient's data is organized into individual XML files named `<documentType>_<documentId>.xml`, grouped in folders by patient ID. The XML includes common structural elements for document properties, relationships, signatures, and file references.\n\n**Document types (70+ forms):** The export covers an impressively broad range of wound care-specific data:\n\n- **Patient information:** PatientDocument (demographics, care team, emergency contact, insurance)\n- **Treatment courses / conditions:** HBOTreatmentCourseDocument, NonWoundConditionDocument, StomaDocument, WoundDocument\n- **Encounter documentation (55+ types):** AllergyListDocument, AncillaryServiceDocument, BiopsyDocument, ChiefComplaintDocument, CompressionTherapyDocument, CustomForm1-10Documents, DebridementDocument, DermalMatrixSubstituteDocument, DischargeInfoDocument, FallRiskDocument, GeneralVisitNotesDocument, HBODocument, HBOPreTreatmentEvaluationDocument, HBOSafetyChecklistDocument, HPIDocument, HROSDocument, ImmunizationsDocument, IncisionAndDrainageDocument, LowerExtremityDocument, MultiDisciplinaryCarePlanDocument, NeuropathyDocument, NH_EducationAssessmentDocument, NPWTApplicationDocument/MaintenanceDocument, NutritionRiskDocument, OstomyPreOperativeDocument, PainAssessmentDocument, PhysicalExamDocument, PhysicianOrdersDocument, PrescriptionDocument, PressureUlcerRiskDocument, ProblemListDocument, ProgressNoteDocument, SkinPerfusionPressureDocument, SuperBillDocument, TCOMDocument, TopicalGrowthFactorDocument, TotalContactCastDocument, VitalSignsDocument, WoundAssessmentDocument, WoundTreatmentNotesDocument, and more\n- **Non-encounter documentation:** CustomScanDocument, PatientCommunicationDocument, TestResultDocument\n\n**Document relationships:** The PDF documents a well-structured parent-child relationship model using PatientID, VisitID, and ParentDocumentID, allowing reconstruction of: patient → encounter → condition/treatment course → clinical observations and procedures.\n\n**Photos and files:** Wound photos and scanned documents are exported via time-limited Azure Blob Storage URLs (valid 90 days from export), referenced within the XML via `<FileReferences>` elements.\n\n**Critical gap — companion Data Dictionary not available:** The PDF explicitly states: \"A companion document, EHIExport – Data Dictionary, provides a data dictionary and identifies the elements within each document.\" This document is not linked on the certification page and could not be found publicly. Without it, there is no field-level documentation for any of the 70+ document types. The main PDF describes only the common structural elements (DocumentProperties, relationships, signatures, file references) — not the specific clinical content fields within each document type.\n\n### Secondary: FHIR g(10) Documentation (40 pages)\n\nThe FHIR API documentation covers the standard US Core / USCDI data classes (g(10) compliance): AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, DocumentReference, Encounter, Goal, Condition, Immunization, Laboratory results, Medication/MedicationRequest, Patient demographics, Procedure, Provenance, Observation, ImplantableDevice, Location, Organization, Practitioner, VitalSigns. It also supports querying all patient data as a C-CDA document.\n\nThis is clearly a **separate system from the b(10) export** — the g(10) uses standard FHIR R4 with US Core profiles, while the b(10) uses proprietary XML. The two are not conflated, which is a positive sign.\n\n### Secondary: Integration API Documentation (4 PDFs, 2019-era)\n\nThese older PDFs document a pre-FHIR integration API for i-heal:\n- **RegistrationHelpv1.pdf** (51 pages) — vendor registration process for API access\n- **AuthenticationHelpv1.pdf** (28 pages) — user auth, password management, facility authorization\n- **PatientSearchHelpv1.pdf** (22 pages) — patient search and demographics access\n- **DataRequestHelpv1.pdf** (8 pages) — C-CDA retrieval API (Common Clinical Data Set per visit)\n\nThe DataRequest API provides C-CDA exports filtered by date range and selectable data parts (allergies, encounters, immunizations, medications, problems, procedures, vitals, etc.). This is a predecessor/complement to the g(10) FHIR API and covers the same clinical summary scope — it is not a substitute for the broader b(10) export.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe b(10) export documentation describes an export that appears to cover the **full breadth of data stored in i-heal**, not just USCDI. This is one of the better (b)(10) implementations in terms of scope.\n\n**Clearly covered (based on document type names):**\n- Patient demographics (PatientDocument)\n- Wound assessments and wound data (WoundDocument, WoundAssessmentDocument, WoundTreatmentNotesDocument)\n- Wound photographs (via FileReferences in WoundAssessmentDocument and others)\n- HBO (hyperbaric oxygen) therapy data (HBODocument, HBOTreatmentCourseDocument, HBOPreTreatmentEvaluationDocument, HBOSafetyChecklistDocument, HBOScreeningChecklistDocument)\n- Non-wound conditions and treatment (NonWoundConditionDocument, NonWoundConditionAssessmentDocument, NonWoundTreatmentNotesDocument)\n- Ostomy/stoma care (StomaDocument, StomaAssessmentDocument, StomaTreatmentNotesDocument, OstomyPreOperativeDocument)\n- Vital signs (VitalSignsDocument)\n- Allergies (AllergyListDocument)\n- Problem list (ProblemListDocument)\n- Immunizations (ImmunizationsDocument)\n- Prescriptions (PrescriptionDocument)\n- Physician orders (PhysicianOrdersDocument)\n- Lab/test results (TestResultDocument)\n- Progress notes (ProgressNoteDocument)\n- Physical exam (PhysicalExamDocument)\n- History of present illness (HPIDocument)\n- Chief complaint (ChiefComplaintDocument)\n- Discharge information and instructions (DischargeInfoDocument, DischargeInstructionsDocument)\n- Care plans (MultiDisciplinaryCarePlanDocument)\n- Patient education (NH_EducationAssessmentDocument, NH_PatientCaregiverEducationDocument)\n- Fall risk and pressure ulcer risk assessments (FallRiskDocument, PressureUlcerRiskDocument)\n- Nutrition risk assessment (NutritionRiskDocument)\n- Pain assessment (PainAssessmentDocument)\n- Neuropathy assessment (NeuropathyDocument)\n- Lower extremity assessment (LowerExtremityDocument)\n- Skin perfusion pressure (SkinPerfusionPressureDocument)\n- Biopsy records (BiopsyDocument)\n- Debridement records (DebridementDocument)\n- Compression therapy (CompressionTherapyDocument)\n- Negative pressure wound therapy (NPWTApplicationDocument, NPWTMaintenanceDocument)\n- Dermal matrix/skin substitute (DermalMatrixSubstituteDocument)\n- Topical growth factor (TopicalGrowthFactorDocument)\n- Total contact cast (TotalContactCastDocument)\n- Incision and drainage (IncisionAndDrainageDocument)\n- Other procedures (OtherProcedureDocument)\n- Custom forms (CustomForm1-10Documents — up to 10 facility-customizable forms)\n- Scanned documents (CustomScanDocument)\n- Patient communications (PatientCommunicationDocument)\n- Billing/superbill (SuperBillDocument)\n- Electronic signatures on all signable documents\n- Insurance information (within PatientDocument)\n- Clinic level of care (ClinicLevelOfCareDocument)\n- TCOM (TCOMDocument — likely a standardized wound healing outcome measure)\n- Ancillary services (AncillaryServiceDocument)\n- HROS (HROSDocument — likely a healing rate/outcome score)\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Detailed billing/claims data beyond the superbill:** The SuperBillDocument is included, but it's unclear whether detailed charge records, payment postings, and claims adjudication data are exported. Given that i-heal operates within hospital systems (billing likely handled by the host hospital's system), this may not be stored in i-heal at all.\n- **Scheduling/appointment data:** No document type for appointment scheduling. This is classified as operational data and is not an EHI gap.\n- **Patient portal messages and patient-generated health data:** PatientCommunicationDocument may cover this, but unclear if patient-submitted data from the portal is included.\n- **Referral data:** No explicit referral document type, though this information may be embedded in discharge or care plan documents.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **proprietary XML format** with a well-defined structural convention. This is an appropriate and honest approach for a (b)(10) export:\n\n- It is **not FHIR** — it does not pretend that wound care specialty data fits neatly into US Core profiles. This is correct; much of i-heal's wound-specific data (HBO treatments, wound measurements, compression therapy, debridement details, etc.) has no standard FHIR representation.\n- The XML files are **machine-readable** and parseable, with consistent naming and relationship conventions.\n- **File references** (for photos/scanned documents) use time-limited Azure Blob Storage URLs, which means exported archives are not fully self-contained — photo access expires after 90 days.\n- Relationships between documents are expressed through common IDs (PatientID, VisitID, ParentDocumentID), which would allow a third party to reconstruct the clinical record hierarchy.\n\nHowever, without the companion Data Dictionary, it is impossible to know the exact XML schema for each document type — what fields are present, what their data types are, and what values they can take.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The EHI Export documentation is clearly written and focused on the (b)(10) requirement specifically — it is not a repackaged FHIR API doc.\n- The structural conventions (document properties, relationships, signatures, file references) are well-documented with XML examples.\n- The parent-child relationship hierarchy is clearly diagrammed.\n- The document types are enumerated with brief descriptions indicating their clinical purpose.\n- The distinction between encounter documentation and non-encounter documentation is clear.\n- The FHIR g(10) API is documented separately and clearly, avoiding the common (b)(10)/(g)(10) conflation.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- The **companion Data Dictionary is missing** — it is referenced as providing \"a data dictionary and identifies the elements within each document\" but is not publicly available. This is a critical gap. Without it, a third party cannot implement an import of this data.\n- There are **no field-level definitions** for any of the 70+ document types in the available documentation. We know a WoundAssessmentDocument exists but not what fields it contains (wound dimensions? etiology codes? healing status? measurement method?).\n- There are **no sample export files** or example XML content for individual document types (only the DocumentProperties wrapper is shown).\n- There is **no XSD or schema definition** for the XML format.\n- There are **no value set definitions** for coded fields.\n- The 90-day expiration on photo/file URLs is a significant limitation for long-term archival, and this is disclosed but not mitigated.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Entity-level:** Excellent — 70+ document types covering the full breadth of wound care clinical data, billing, and specialty-specific assessments.\n- **Field-level:** Unknown — entirely dependent on the missing companion Data Dictionary.\n- **Relationship documentation:** Good — parent-child relationships are clearly mapped.\n- **Value sets:** Unknown — no coded field documentation available.\n- **Versioning:** None evident. The PDF is dated November 2023 with no version history.\n\n## Assessment Summary\n\nHealogics/Net Health has done **genuinely thoughtful work on b(10) compliance**. The export is clearly a real, purpose-built mechanism — not a repackaged FHIR API or C-CDA export. It covers 70+ wound care-specific document types in a proprietary XML format, which is the right approach for specialty clinical data that doesn't map to standard FHIR resources.\n\nHowever, the **companion Data Dictionary is the missing critical piece**. The available documentation describes the envelope (how files are structured, named, and related) but not the content (what clinical fields are in each document type). Without field-level definitions, a third party cannot meaningfully use or import the exported data.\n\n**Grade: B-** — Strong architectural approach and impressive scope of document types, significantly undermined by the missing field-level documentation that would make the export actually usable by a recipient.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.healogics.com/certified-ehr-technology/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (all files linked from a single flat page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The companion \"EHIExport – Data Dictionary\" document referenced in the main PDF is not linked on the certification page and could not be found via URL probing, web search, or Wayback Machine.
